# Decisions

Format: **D-###: title**, with context · choice · how to reverse · status. Every decision is **provisional** until the owner rules on it. Nothing here claims owner approval unless it quotes a dated owner ruling.

| ID | Decision | How to reverse | Status |
|---|---|---|---|

| D-002 | Dodge modifier ⌊(DEX−dexPivot)/2⌋ with the shipped `dexPivot = 10` (always negative on the lean scale). Flagged for balance review | `rules/dodge.json#dexPivot` | provisional (superseded for Core 2 by D-1420) |

| D-006 | Audio: generated music in the 8 context file slots. The procedural synth is the fallback and handles UI SFX | `audio/contexts.json` | provisional |

| D-008 | WebGL build was deferred (UI Toolkit WebGL renderer bug in 6000.6.0f1: `CopyBufferRanges`). **Amended by D-1250: no longer deferred**; the editor is 6000.6.6f1, whose release notes do not mention the bug, so the first WebGL build must confirm it. Windows first, then WebGL, then Android | Build profiles | provisional |
| D-009 | Single-use places end the visit on any committed service (shipped rule); the reference preset makes places multi-use. The validator guarantees an exit | `places.json`, settings | provisional |
| D-010 | Weapon-set swap lives in an Armaments popover opened from the player figure | UXML | provisional |
| D-011 | Relics without paintings get generated icons; typographic sigils are the fallback | registry | provisional |
| D-012 | Unity project at `Unity/`, tools at `Tools/`, engine-free tests at `Tests/Domain.Tests` | Repo layout | provisional |
| D-013 | Potions sit in the combat footer and the map corner. In rooms they are reached through Menu → Potions | UXML | provisional |
| D-014 | Extract moves the card into the deck, as shipped | `rules/smithing.json` | provisional |
| D-015 | RUN_HUD XP strip behind `ui/runHud.json#showXp`, off by default | Flag | provisional |
| D-016 | Two PanelSettings (wide 1280×720, narrow 430×780), with `minPhysical` enforced after scaling | `layout.json` | provisional |
| D-017 | If the command log can't be replayed (version or content changed), resume from the snapshot and warn | SaveService | provisional |
| D-018 | Converted PNGs are gitignored and their `.meta` files committed. `Tools/import-assets` regenerates them from `D:\repos\AshenSpire` | `.gitignore` | provisional |
| D-019 | The owner's reference config (`docs/design/reference/ashen-spire-game-config.json`) is the default preset; `shipped` stays available | `settings/presets` | provisional |
| D-020 | The reference config hides prologue Pause and Skip. Hold-to-skip stays on the pad and keyboard Back action | `prologue.json` | provisional |
| D-021 | Feature lanes are `feature/<feature>/main`; story branches are `feature/<feature>/<story>` (git can't hold both `feature/x` and `feature/x/y`) | Branch names | provisional |
| D-022 | `E.F.S.P` versioning from 0.0.1.0. Only merges into `dev` bump the version; `main` is tagged on promotion | `Tools/version.mjs` | provisional |
| D-023 | **No Unity MCP bridge in this session.** All Unity work runs through batchmode via `Tools/unity.sh` (§6.2). The MCP package is added only for future sessions | Add MCP later | provisional |
| D-024 | The machine's UPM `cacheRoot` points at an unavailable drive (`H:\…`). `Tools/unity.sh` sets `UPM_CACHE_ROOT` to the per-user Unity cache for batchmode runs. The global config is not modified | Unset the env var | provisional |
| D-025 | SVG import uses the built-in `com.unity.modules.vectorgraphics` module of 6000.6. The Edge and Blender rasterizers are fallbacks | `manifest.json` | provisional |
| D-026 | Minimal package set; Unity's default template packages (IAP, analytics, XR, multiplayer center, timeline, navigation) were removed | `manifest.json` | provisional |
| D-027 | Application-layer namespace is `Ashen.App` (assembly `Ashen.Application`), so it can't shadow `UnityEngine.Application` inside `Ashen.*` code | Rename the namespace | provisional |
| D-028 | Codegen is a Node tool (`Tools/codegen.mjs`) instead of an Editor asmdef. It runs without a Unity licence in CI and can't break on a domain reload | Port it to the Editor | provisional |
| D-029 | The owner reference config is imported through the shipped strict importer. A data-driven legacy key map handles renames. Only values it rejects (out of range, or unknown in the shipped build) are set aside and applied directly, and they are recorded in the preset | `Tools/export/legacy-key-map.json` | provisional |
| D-030 | Content IDs stay as shipped (camelCase), because web configs, saves and the oracle use them. New IDs use lowerCamelCase. This amends 08 §3 (`lower_snake_case`) | Add an ID migration with aliases | provisional |
| D-031 | Repo-local `nuget.config` clears machine sources (this machine maps `nuget.org` to `C:\Python\Lib`) and keeps packages in the gitignored `.cache/nuget`. The Unity UPM cache also moved to `.cache/upm` because **C: is full (0 bytes)** | Delete `nuget.config` | provisional |
| D-032 | Tests are shared engine-free NUnit sources in `Unity/Assets/Tests/EditMode/Shared`. They run under Unity EditMode **and** `dotnet test` (`Tests/Domain.Tests`) from one codebase | — | provisional |
| D-033 | us-0.5 lands in two parts. **Part 1 (now):** named streams, bit-exact parity with the shipped RNG, seed codec and seat order. **Part 2:** golden replays and map-generation parity land with the map generator (F3) and the combat loop (F2), which they need. F0's "map oracle" exit item moves to F3 | Track in BUILD-LOG | provisional |
| D-034 | PRNG algorithm constants are generated numbers (`RngAlgorithm`, from `codegen.config.json` numberSets), not content. They define the algorithm and aren't tuning. Stream names and the seed alphabet are content (`rules/rng.json`) | — | provisional |
| D-035 | **Import by phase (disk-constrained).** The registry covers **all** 5,265 source assets from metadata (IDs, dimensions, alpha, SHA-256) with no conversion. Only the ID prefixes listed in `Tools/assets.config.json#import` are converted into Unity per phase. F0 pilot: 899 assets (UI flasks, class sprites, enemies with states, poses and painted art, VFX, backgrounds, environments, map, relics, equipment icons, defeated poses). Animations (3,071), outfits, poses and the prologue import in F1/F2/F3, as needed and as disk allows. D: has 3.7 GB free and C: 0.2 GB | Add prefixes to `import` | provisional |
| D-036 | Combat arithmetic uses IEEE-754 `double`, in the shipped engine's operation order, so results match bit-for-bit. Domain avoids transcendental functions; basic double ops are deterministic on x64/IL2CPP. Amends 08 §5 for the combat engine | Switch to fixed point with oracle re-validation | provisional |
| D-037 | The combat state is a JSON document model (`JObject`) that mirrors the shipped snapshot format. That gives field parity and makes snapshots, saves and oracle comparisons trivial. Keys come from generated constants | Introduce typed wrappers | provisional |
| D-038 | The shipped prototype 'foundation' ruleset (null in shipped play) is not ported; it stays a seam | Port `combatRules.js` | provisional |
| D-039 | **Owner ruling (2026-09-26): nothing is 1.x yet.** 1.0.0.0–1.0.0.3 are renumbered 0.1.1.1–0.1.1.4 and tag v1.0.0.0 is removed. Phase completion is a promotion, not an epic bump. `bump epic` requires `ASHEN_OWNER_RELEASE=1` | Owner declares a release | owner ruling |
| D-040 | **Generated content keeps the shipped key order.** Object order carries meaning in the rules: weighted enemy move picks walk `moves` in order, and reward pools and RNG picks walk registry order. `Tools/transform-content.mjs` no longer sorts keys; only string tables and the manifest stay sorted. `rules/rowOrder.json` (us-0.3) is now redundant with this, but it is kept consistent | Sort, and make order explicit (list-valued moves) | provisional |
| D-041 | The combat engine lives in `Ashen.Domain.Combat`, a line-faithful port of the shipped `engine/combat.js` family: queue and turn loop, actions, statuses, triggers and predicates, formulas, ratings, hand rules, property mounts, and card resolution. It reads `CombatData` (the runtime registry tables plus `rules/mechanics.json` and the new `rules/combatEngine.json`, which holds the limits, defaults and vocabulary maps the shipped code kept as constants). Names come from `Tools/codegen.d/combat.json`. **Deferred, each throwing by name if reached:** smithed card faces, restoring a fight without combat ratings, skill-XP receipts (no effect on combat outcomes), co-op seats, and the foundation ruleset (D-038). **Closed by us-5.11:** restoring (and starting) a fight without combat ratings (D-119); the mid-fight equipment intents and createCombat's profile-snapshot and swap-rule fallbacks, which the first port refused (D-116–D-120). Co-op seats and the foundation ruleset stay deferred | Port the deferred paths in their stories | provisional |
| D-042 | Run creation lives in `Ashen.Domain.Run`, a line-faithful port of the shipped `createRunState` family: creation stats and presets, derived-stat rules and the host snapshot, the equipment profile snapshot, kit plan and receipts, weapon card packages and the attack plan, the composed starting deck and item-owned cards (mounts), `stampDeck`, relic modifiers, flask charges and growth, zones, and the seat order. The run is the shipped JSON document (D-037). It reads `RunData`, which is the combat data plus the creation tables (dumped by `Tools/oracle-run.mjs`, shipped insertion order per D-040), `rules/handRules.json` and the new `rules/runEngine.json`. That file holds the versions, defaults and vocabulary lists the shipped code kept as constants. Names come from `Tools/codegen.d/run.json` (`Tools/run-keys.mjs`), which reuses `CombatKeys`/`CombatValues` wherever the combat fragment already has the name. `RunCombat.CreateArgs` builds the `createCombat` arguments `main.js enterCombat` passes for an ordinary run | Port further paths in their stories | provisional |
| D-043 | **Deferred run-creation paths, each throwing `NotSupportedException` by name:** fights inside a World Journey, a legacy dungeon or a Custom Climb (`combatMods` chaos and Endless). **Closed by us-5.11** (D-121, D-122): a custom attribute allocation (`normalizeRunAttributes`), `derivedStatOptions` override layers, being born under a saved derived-stat snapshot, restoring a run's derived-stat snapshot and the schema migrations that heal missing pool ledgers (`RunState.RestoreDerivedStats`), and `gameConfig.handRules.*` settings overrides. Supported creation options: `attributeMode`, `attributes`, `derivedStatOptions`, `derivedStatRuleSnapshot`, `startingKitId`, `startingHands`, `startingArmourId`, `startingRelicId` and `profileMeta` | Port each in the story that needs it | provisional |
| D-044 | The heal ledger (`model/healLedger.js`) is not ported. It is a non-enumerable witness on the run that records what each writer replaced; no value or save byte depends on it. The `why` sentences of `flaskGrowthPlan` are not ported either: they are presentation and are not stored on the run | Port with the diagnostics/tools story | provisional |
| D-045 | Run parity compares documents strictly on presence: an absent key and an explicit `null` differ, because the shipped document drops `undefined` and keeps `null`. Numbers compare as doubles and key order is ignored. The run oracle also records the combat-start snapshot and RNG counters that each recorded argument set produces, so the C# run → args → `CombatStart.Create` chain is held to the shipped start end to end | Loosen to the projection compare | provisional |
| D-046 | The act map lives in `Ashen.Domain.Map`, a line-faithful port of the shipped `engine/mapgen.js`, `model/floorplan.js` (resolver and run shape), `engine/actmap.js` (`buildActMap`, `bossEncounterForNode`, `drawSeatOrder`), `resolveUnknownNode`/`rollEncounter`, the quest-history gate, `encounterFitsSeat`/`finalTier` and `bossDestinationLabel`. Same draw order, IEEE doubles, JS `Math.round` (floor of x + 0.5), and insertion order everywhere; a JS object's integer keys (`plan.fixed`) iterate ascending. It reads `MapData` (encounters, events, enemies, seats, mapConfigs, quest gates, boss locations, run-shape limits, legacy bosses, balance.endless), built from the oracle dump or `RuntimeRegistries.ToMapData` | Port again from the JS if the shipped rules change | provisional |
| D-047 | The map graph is a typed model (`ActMapGraph`/`MapNode`) that serializes to the shipped JSON key order (nodes, startIds, shrineId, bossId, floors, columns, bossIds; node id, floor, col, type, next, resolved or encounterId/destinationLabel). Configs stay JSON documents (D-037) because the resolver validates arbitrary authored shapes | Hold the graph as a `JObject` | provisional |
| D-048 | `rules/mapEngine.json` holds what the shipped map code kept as constants: typing retries (40), the door guard (50), the walker step (−1..1), the shortest act (2), node types, anchor kinds, Unknown kinds, run-shape keys, the node-id spelling, the boss pool, the boss-label separators and the event-choice history grammar. The id pattern is matched whole, so a trailing newline fails as it does in JS | Edit the rules file (every seed changes) | provisional |
| D-049 | The floor-plan readout (`describePlan`) is tooling text and is not ported. Refusals keep the shipped keys (the parity test compares keys); their messages are developer-facing templates in `MapMessages`. The two player-facing run-shape notes are returned structured (type, count) so the Custom Climb screen can word them from string tables | Port the readout | provisional |
| D-050 | `drawSeatOrder` reuses `Ashen.Domain.Run.SeatOrder` (already oracle-verified); `ActMap.DrawSeatOrder` only adapts `MapData.Seats` | — | provisional |
| D-051 | Boss destination labels are composed in the domain exactly as shipped ("location · enemy & enemy") from `catalog/bossDestinations.json`, enemy names and the separators in `rules/mapEngine.json`, and saved on the graph. Localising them is left to the presentation layer | Resolve labels at display time from string keys | provisional |
| D-052 | **The press-any-input gate is a state of `screen:title`** (`ui/screens.json#title.gate`), not its own screen: 09 Appendix C has no gate id, and the wordmark stays in place while the gate lifts. Any key, button, click or tap lifts it (`InputSystem.onAnyButtonPress` plus pointer down); focus then lands on the first enabled row, and Submit is ignored for the `gateSettle` token so the lifting key cannot also press a row | Register a `gate` screen and a transition | provisional |
| D-053 | **One input source for the focus model:** `Ashen.Platform.InputRouter` reads the Input System's built-in UI actions (Navigate, Submit, Cancel, plus Tab) and drives the Navigator. UI Toolkit's own navigation events are swallowed, and a Submit passes only when the router sent it, so nothing is handled twice. Reason: UI Toolkit's InputForUI delivered no navigation events in batchmode PlayMode runs. Active Input Handling is now **Input System** (`activeInputHandler: 1`, as 09 §2 required; it was still the old Input Manager). Pointer input stays with UI Toolkit | Remove the router and let UI Toolkit navigate natively | provisional |
| D-054 | **UI key sets are hand-listed in `Tools/codegen.d/ui.json`** (ScreenIds, UiNames, UiClasses, UiKeys, UiValues, menu actions and rules, confirm ids, placeholders, token keys, resource paths). `UiDataTests` keep them in step with `ui/screens.json`, the UXML `name=` attributes, `ui/menus.json` and `ui/tokens.json`. This replaces generating UiNames from UXML (08 §6) for now | Generate the sets from UXML and screens.json | provisional |
| D-055 | **`Tokens.uss` is generated by `Tools/ui-tokens.mjs`** from `ui/tokens.json` (groups → `--<prefix>-<key>` with units). Its `--check` mode also lints every other USS file: no raw colours, no lengths, no numbers except 0 and 1. `Tools/ci/check-content.mjs` runs it, and `UiDataTests.StyleSheetsUseTokenVariablesOnly` repeats the lint inside the test suites | Move it into codegen.mjs | provisional |
| D-056 | **UI art comes from a `UiArtCatalog` asset in Resources**, built by `Cli.BuildUiArt` from `ui/components.json#art.include` and `assets/registry.json` (22 textures: the two backgrounds and the 20 class sprites). Textures are referenced by GUID, so the gitignored PNGs resolve again once the pipeline regenerates them (D-018). This is a stand-in until Addressables (`BuildAddressables`) lands | Switch to Addressables keys from the registry | provisional |
| D-057 | **Both PanelSettings are created at runtime** from `ui/layout.json` (wide 1280×720, narrow 430×780, Expand) and swapped on the one UIDocument by layout mode. The theme is a Resources TSS that imports Unity's default runtime theme (default font, D-005), `Tokens.uss` and `Base.uss`. `minPhysical` is enforced after scaling by the Navigator: `.kit-touch` gets a minimum size, and `.kit-text-value`/`.kit-text-header` get a minimum font size, both in reference px (minimum ÷ panel scale) | Author PanelSettings assets and change them by hand | provisional |
| D-058 | **Screens not built yet are registered as `planned`** in `ui/screens.json` (creation, actMap, journal, customRun, settings, lobby). Their title rows (Journal, Custom Run, Settings) are disabled by the `targetBuilt` rule. Continue, Load and New open their W2 doors, and confirming ends in a Refusal ("The climb screens arrive in a later build") until creation and the run screens exist. US-1.3's "reaches everything" is therefore only partly met | Flip `planned` off as each screen lands | provisional |
| D-059 | **Slot summary contract:** a run save's payload carries `summary` {name, classId, portrait (registry id), act, floor, hp, hpMax, seed, savedAt (ISO-8601 UTC), journey, playtimeSeconds} (`SlotSummaryKeys`). The W-02 preview and the W-03 rows read only this object. Continue resumes the Ready slot with the newest `savedAt`. A save from a newer build shows as kept, not loaded; a slot with files but no valid copy shows as unreadable | Read the full run snapshot when F1 combat saves exist | provisional |
| D-060 | **Screen capture is a PlayMode test** (`ScreenCaptureTests`, which runs only when `ASHEN_CAPTURE=1`). `Cli.CaptureScreens` sets that variable and starts it through `TestRunnerApi`, then exits with the result, so it is run **without `-quit`** (and never with `-nographics`). Fixtures live in `Tests/Fixtures/screens/*.json`; sizes come from `ui/layout.json#verification`. The PNGs are written as RGB (about 21 MB for 52) and committed | Capture in edit mode, or keep the screenshots out of git | provisional |
| D-061 | **W2 doors are data in `ui/menus.json#confirms`** (title, body, occupied body, primary, back, policy, hold, single). The destructive tone comes from the policy level in `ui/confirmationPolicies.json` (`policy.deleteSave` is DESTRUCTIVE: red primary with hold-to-confirm). The holdMs is the `holdConfirm` token (1100). A dev-only `kitGallery` screen shows every kit component with fixture values for SHOOT and REVIEW | Split confirms into `ui/confirms.json` | provisional |
| D-062 | The post-combat pipeline lives in `Ashen.Domain.Rewards`, a line-faithful port of the run-writing half of the shipped `main.js onCombatEnd`: the write-back from the fight, `applySkillXp`/`awardSkillXp` with drafts and the auto-upgrade rule (`model/skills.js`), `awardClassXp` and the draft pool (`model/classTree.js`), `combatLevelXp`/`awardLevelXp` with the pool re-derivation (`model/levelup.js`), `combatXpGains`, `stampDeck`, `grantSmithingReward`, the reward rolls of `engine/encounters.js` (cinders, card offer, skill and class drafts, flask pity, relics, armament drops) on the shipped streams and draw order, the boss ledgers, the summit check and `beginPendingReward`. The entry point is `CombatEnd.Apply(data, run, combat, enc, result, rng, options)`. It returns a `CombatEndReceipt` (outcome `defeat`, `victory` or `reward`, plus the ledgers' awards, the Smithing Stone receipt and the rewards offered). The shipped code discards some of these values; the receipt keeps them so the caller does not have to derive them again. `RewardsData` is `RunData` plus the tag tree (`registries.nodes`), `balance/customRun.json` `ASCENSION_ORDER` (for `activeMods`) and the new `rules/rewardsEngine.json`, which holds defaults, vocabulary lists and the victory titles. Names come from `Tools/codegen.d/rewards.json` (`Tools/rewards-keys.mjs`). That fragment reuses the combat and run names and fails on a duplicate. Custom Climb Chaos Rewards and Endless are supported | Port further paths in their stories | provisional |
| D-063 | The fight and the run share one loadout: shipped `createCombat` keeps `run.loadout` by reference, and `enterCombat` rejoins a restored fight's copy. `CombatEnd.Apply` copies `combat.Loadout` onto the run when the two are different objects. Under shipped aliasing this does nothing, and it keeps a mid-fight equipment change once the C# combat ports the swap commands (ported by us-5.11, D-116) | Drop the copy once the session shares the object | provisional |
| D-064 | Rewards parity (`Tools/oracle-rewards.mjs`, `RewardsParityTests`). A JS harness mirrors `main.js` lines 2279–2456, `rollDrop`, `victoryTitle`, `endlessOn` and `chaosRewardsOn`, and calls the real shipped functions in order. Cases: the 60 golden combats replayed to their end, plus 108 generated fights (class × door × 18 run variants). Every case self-checks that its recorded snapshot restores to an identical pipeline. Generated elite and boss fights scale enemy HP by 0.2 and 0.1 so the bot can win. One variant sets the combat's `equipmentChanged` flag directly, because the C# combat has no swap commands yet (ported since, D-116). The comparison is stricter than D-045: key order must match as well as presence and values. The RNG counters after the fight must also match | Loosen to the D-045 compare | provisional |
| D-065 | `grantSmithingReward` normalizes only `balance.smithing.rewardByPool`, which is what it reads. The shipped `normalizeSmithingRules` also validates the smith's `services` table; that check belongs to the smithing story. The two differ only on malformed content, which the shipped version refuses and this one accepts | Port `normalizeServices` with the smith | provisional |
| D-066 | **Deferred, throwing `NotSupportedException` (`RewardsMessages.JourneyCombatEndDeferred`):** fights inside a World Journey or a legacy dungeon. This covers `completeJourneyNode`, `resolveDungeonNode`, the journey summit (`currentNodeId === anchors.final`), the dungeon suffix on the Smithing reward id and the `after: 'map'` door for them. D-043 already defers entering such fights | Port with the journey/dungeon story | provisional |
| D-067 | **Left to the caller, because they write no run field:** music and SFX, `sendLanStatus`, the victory beat, `persist`/`saves.clearRun`, `finishRun`, `mountGameOver` and `mountRewards`, and `collectArmament`. `finishRun` records the run result to the profile (`runResult`, `recordResult`, `recordProgress`, `evaluateUnlocks`); `collectArmament` is the reward screen's take path. The receipt's outcome tells the caller which of these to run. The profile's `found` set and the level-up dial (`resolveLevelUpValue`) are inputs (`CombatEndOptions`) | Port with the Application reward/game-over flow | provisional |
| D-068 | The boss title uses `ToUpperInvariant`. JS `toUpperCase` gives a different result only outside ASCII, and every shipped enemy name is ASCII. The skill and level curves use `Math.Pow` against V8's `Math.pow`; they agree on every recorded case. A content change that brings non-ASCII names or new curve constants needs the oracle re-run | Culture-aware casing if content needs it | provisional |
| D-069 | **Run and rewards data are built from content, row for row and key for key.** `RuntimeRegistries.ToRunData` and `ToRewardsData` build what the run and post-combat ports read (creation modes, seats, encounters, characterCreation with stamped keepsakes, attributeRules, derivedStatRules, tagFamilies, the tag tree `nodes`, and the manifest's contentVersion via `RunSnapshot.ContentVersion`). Rows are copied verbatim into run documents, so key order is part of parity (D-040). Two ordering fixes make content-built rows equal the shipped registries exactly: the transform records each extracted display-text field's anchor (the key it followed) in the generated `rules/textAnchors.json`, and `RegistryBundle` re-inserts text there; `TagStamp` writes `tags` before `propertyTags`/`kindIds` for non-equipment rows, as the shipped `{ ...def, tags, ...conferred }` does. The only order not held is the top level of the equipment object, which is read by name and never iterated or copied. | Content-built data replays every recorded run (76), combat start (152) and post-combat case (168) exactly; the content tests now compare rows strictly, key order included. |
| D-070 | **A run save is one SaveService envelope around a versioned run payload** (`Ashen.App.Run.RunSession`): `formatVersion` (`rules/runFlow.json#saveFormat`), `presetId`, the D-059 `summary`, `name`, `portrait`, `playtimeSeconds`, the run document, and while a fight is on `location`, `encounterId` and the combat checkpoint. Commands between checkpoints go to the generation's log with their after-state hash (PF-06). Load: a newer save is kept (Newer); no valid copy, another format or a payload that cannot be restored is Damaged and takes the kit's unreadable-slot refusal; a changed content hash resumes from the checkpoint without the log, and a diverged log stops at the last verified command, both with a warning (D-017), and the slot is re-committed at once so it holds one clean generation | Migrate payload formats with `SaveService.RegisterMigration` | provisional |
| D-071 | **`rules/runFlow.json` holds the F1 run flow:** the preselected class, default tint and portrait template, the journey written to the summary and the default name key; the first fight as a rule (encounters of seat `weald`, pool `normal`, in authoring order, at ordinal 1: the two-enemy patrol, so targeting has a choice); and the autosave policy: `afterCommand: append` (every accepted command is appended to the log) plus a full checkpoint on `endTurn` (the PF-06 "combat turn end" commit point), on fight end and on Save & quit | Edit the rules file | provisional |
| D-072 | **The F1 end of a fight is a state of `screen:combat`** (like the D-052 gate), not a new screen id: Victory/You perished, "saved in slot n", the planned note, a disabled [Rewards] (`screen:rewards` is registered `planned`, D-058) and [Return to title]. The finished fight stays checkpointed, so Continue reopens the end state. Planned ids added: `rewards`, `pileViewer`, `armoury`, `runEnd` (Appendix C) | Replace with W-08 when rewards land | provisional |
| D-073 | **The reference preset makes the Reaver unable to begin:** its `startingFlaskAllocation {hp: 2}` merges over `{hp: 3, mana: 1}` and fails the shipped rule hp + mana = capacity 4 (`createFlaskCharges`). The port keeps the shipped refusal. W-04 validates each class once (`RunContent.ClassProblem`), shows the Reaver with "cannot begin with the current tuning" and refuses Begin; the preselection falls back from the data default (Reaver) to the first class that can begin (Starseer). **Owner to rule** on the reference tuning (e.g. `mana: 2`) | Fix the preset patch; the fallback then stops firing | needs owner |
| D-074 | **Screen states have their own focus orders:** `ui/screens.json#focusModes` (combat: targeting → targets; discard → hand + discard footer; flasks → panel; enemyTurn → footer group; ended → end panel). `IScreenView.FocusMode` picks one; FocusModel walks it. Pad Start is a new router intent (`Menu`): run screens open W-20 and W-20 resumes. Escape backs out of the innermost combat state first, else opens W-20 | Model states as modal layers | provisional |

A new build with another profile hash therefore does not refuse an old run. The door refuses only when the registries it is handed carry another hash than the run's pin: that is the recoverable status `profile-mismatch`, the slot kept as it is (not archived, not migrated), and a pinned snapshot whose own hash does not verify is refused as `could not be restored`. `CoreRunRules.Create` stamps the same snapshot, `CoreRunRules.PinOf` reads its id and hash (the D-167 run record) and `CoreRunRules.MatchesProfile` is the door's guard. | D-210 | **The Core 2 hit is statics over the combat documents: `CoreHitRoll`, `CoreProposal`, `CoreAvoidance` (WP-6 of docs/design/11).** `Domain/Combat/CoreHitRoll.cs` ports `attackHitPreview`, `resolveAttackHit`, `previewCardAttackHit`, `effectiveEvasion`, `evadeStackGain`, `precisionStatMix`, `precisionEntryGrant` (`stanceHit.js`) with `attributesOf` and `levelOf` (`counterReturn.js`); `CoreAvoidance.cs` ports `chanceMode`, `effectiveChance`, `rollChanceDie`, `previewAvoidance` and `resolveAvoidance` (`combatAvoidance.js`); `CoreProposal.cs` ports the part of `proposalTargetPlan` / `proposalTargetRules` a hit reads. Accuracy is `floor(precisionBase + statMix x (Precision stacks + 1) + basePrecisionBonus + maneuverPrecision[maneuver] + proposal.accuracyDelta / 5 + range precisionDelta)`; Evasion is `floor(evasionBase + DEX + baseEvasionBonus + Evade stacks x (baseAC + DEX) + prepared bonus)`; the die needs `Evasion - Accuracy`; the preview enumerates the 20 outcomes (400 with advantage or disadvantage) with the strict natural rule (a 1 always misses, a 20 always hits, the kept die of two decides), so the shown percentage and the roll agree. `Resolve` takes the die as a delegate `(low, high) => int` (the default draws `combatAvoidance` on the Core 2 counter set of D-181), draws nothing when the contact is out of range or cannot be avoided, draws two with advantage or disadvantage, spends the next-Evade mode and a prepared charge when dice were rolled against an attempted Evade, consumes one Evade stack on an in-range miss, and announces `attackHit` or `attackMissed` with the web's payload and label. Only the FixCore path exists in a Core 2 run: the 405 Accuracy (the Evasion table, Sweep's flat bonus) and the 405 distance / Evade avoidance rolls are refused by name (`Core2HitMessages`). The literals the web keeps in code (die 20, natural miss 1, percent 100, the proposal accuracy divisor 5, the Decoy ranges) are the `hit` section of `rules/core2Combat.json` (schema updated, D-183); keys, values and messages are the `core2-hit.json` codegen fragment. `stanceMinimum`, `stanceMaximum` and `stanceAdvantageBonus` of the accuracy block are not read by a hit (the web only filters them when it merges the rules); they price stance damage and belong to WP-7 | Edit `CoreStances` / `CoreActions` (the WP-5 files) or fold the hit into `CoreActions.Execute` | provisional |

| D-221 | **The damage receipt is a pure function and the hit applies it (WP-7).** `CoreDamage.ProfileDamageReceipt` ports `profileDamageReceipt`: base, then AR (or PR for a player's spell or a magic share) x `attackRatingWeight` with one source line per rating the attacker carries (scoped to the piece that lent the card), the matching-stance multiplier, Vulnerable, the proposal status rules (`HitPlan`), then the stance advantage (1.25) or the Smash-against-a-defensive-stance rules (x2 damage, a share of Block added, a share of Poise drained), and the defence mitigation, flooring at each stage; the incoming hit is split into a physical and a magic part (`splitContactParts`), Block takes the physical part, Barrier the magic part, then the Poise and Ward protections (`protectionReceipt` through the kernel: Poise reduces 25% of what Block let through, capped by the pool; with Ward off, Poise takes the magic part too), and the rest is the Health lost. The prefix evaluation the web runs on a detached copy (`candidateState`) is not reproduced: amounts are read from the live state, which differs only when an earlier effect of the same card changes what a later amount reads, and every op in this package's covered set (damage, Block, Barrier, Poise damage, a status) leaves those inputs alone | Port `candidateState` (a structured clone of the fight per card); or run the web's actions as JSON | provisional |

| D-251 | **The stack is LIFO state and the interrupted work is held aside.** `chooseReaction` Play validates the option against a fresh offer, commits it to `reactionStack` (`{ groupKey, entries[], resolving, counterSeats }`; an entry is `{ side, ownerId, optionId, play, cardId, cardInstanceId, maneuver }`) and offers the seat again until it passes, has no option left or holds the cap: 1 under the per-hit contact policy (the Core 2 default), `counterReturnMaxDepth` on a Counter return, else the profile's `chainLimit` (16) over `stack.maxDepth`. When nobody is offered again the stack resolves last pushed first through the same `Before` call: each card is played as a reaction (`CombatState.ReactionPlaying`: off the player's turn, `reactionDepth` 1, the incoming attacker as `reactionSourceId` for Counterstrike and the reaction Sweep) with the queue it interrupted held in `reactionResume` (the state keeps its length; the actions are runtime objects) and handed back by `RestoreReactionQueue` once the card has nothing left to choose; a refused play, a card that left the hand, a second Counter, a gone seat and an ended root fizzle (`combatReactionFizzled`) and pay nothing. A paid Counter keeps its serial on the root (`manualCounterSerials`), so only it answers the hits of a reaction, and its reply carries the reaction depth: the paid reply ends the chain. `deferRootPlay` / `restoreRootPlay` keep a player-card root's play aside while an enemy window waits (units: no shipped enemy window opens in this package) | Resolve each push at once | provisional |

| D-1250 | **Unity 6000.6.6f1 and the WebGL build (owner: "upgrade unity and set up the webgl build").** The pin moves from 6000.6.0f1 to 6000.6.6f1 (changeset `d20c9ac777da`, released 2026-10-15): `ProjectVersion.txt`, `ci.yml` `UNITY_VERSION`, the `Tools/unity.sh` default path, the README and the scope target line. Package versions in `manifest.json` / `packages-lock.json` are left as they are: nothing says 6000.6.6 needs other versions, and the editor re-resolves the bundled packages (URP, UI Toolkit, ugui, the modules) when it first opens the project, so the lock file may change on that first open and should be committed then. `Cli.BuildWebGL` (`Ashen.EditorTools.Cli`, not `Ashen.Editor.Cli`: the namespace the other entry points and CI use) validates the content, sets the WebGL PlayerSettings in code (Brotli, decompression fallback on so GitHub Pages needs no headers, no threads, IndexedDB data cache, explicit exceptions with no stack trace, 256 MB initial and 2 GB maximum memory with geometric growth, managed stripping Low because Newtonsoft reads members by reflection) and builds the enabled scenes to `Builds/WebGL` (or `-customBuildPath`, which game-ci passes). `BuildWindows` is still the F0 stub; WebGL is the first real player build | `Cli.cs`, `ProjectVersion.txt`, `ci.yml`, `docs/BUILD-WEBGL.md` | provisional |
| D-1251 | **A browser cannot read StreamingAssets with System.IO, so the content is fetched at boot.** Every content read already goes through `IContentSource` (the only `File.*` on StreamingAssets is `DirectoryContentSource`). `MemoryContentSource` (Content, engine-free, CRLF normalised like the directory source) holds the files; `WebContentLoader` (Platform) reads `manifest.json` over `UnityWebRequest`, then every file in it (8 at a time), so no directory listing is needed. `AshenBoot` starts that coroutine under `UNITY_WEBGL && !UNITY_EDITOR` and builds the host when it ends; the editor, Windows players and tests still use `DirectoryContentSource` and boot in `Awake` as before. The art is not affected: sprites and the UI trees come from Resources catalogs (serialized into the player), and there is no audio file read yet (the audio directors read data only) | `MemoryContentSource`, `WebContentLoader`, `AshenBoot` | provisional |
| D-1252 | **Saves on WebGL.** `persistentDataPath` is an IndexedDB-backed in-memory file system, so `File` / `FileStream` / `Directory` in `SaveService` work, but (1) writes reach IndexedDB only when the file system is synced: `Plugins/WebGL/AshenFs.jslib` (`FS.syncfs(false)`, one flush at a time) is called by `WebFileSystem` every 5 s, on pause and on losing focus, in addition to whatever Unity does itself (not verified which Unity versions flush on file close; the extra flush is harmless); (2) `File.Replace` is not known to exist in the emscripten file system, so under `UNITY_WEBGL && !UNITY_EDITOR` the atomic replace is copy-to-`.bak`, copy over, delete the temp file (the same end state; a crash between the copies is the only difference, and the corrupt-save recovery already reads `.bak` and the mirror). `Thread.Sleep` in the replace retry is the only thread use in Game code (no `Task.Run`, `System.Threading` types or `new Thread`); on the single-threaded WebGL build it blocks the page for at most the retry delays and only after an `IOException` | `SaveService`, `WebFileSystem`, `AshenFs.jslib` | provisional |
| D-1253 | **CI and Pages.** `ci.yml` gains `build-webgl` (game-ci/unity-builder@v4, `targetPlatform: WebGL`, `Ashen.EditorTools.Cli.BuildWebGL`, skipped with a notice when `UNITY_LICENSE` is unset, same branch condition as `build-windows`, uploads the build as an artifact). `pages-webgl.yml` builds on every push to `dev` and publishes with `actions/upload-pages-artifact` + `actions/deploy-pages` (`pages: write`, `id-token: write`, environment `github-pages`, a `.nojekyll` marker) to https://cehinds.github.io/AshenedSpire/. Neither build restores `Unity/Assets/Art/Imported` (the converted images are gitignored, D-018, and `build-windows` does not restore them either): the CI player has the layout, text and rules but no images until the owner decides where CI gets them (commit them, an LFS budget, or an import step from the web repo). Documented in `docs/BUILD-WEBGL.md` | `ci.yml`, `pages-webgl.yml` | superseded by D-1300 (art in CI) |

A CI run with D-1300 alone would still have lacked the title and combat backgrounds and the class sprites. `--import` now also rebuilds every registry id that has a `unityPath`, no `release` and no file: the same `source` path in the high or common pack (830 are byte-identical to the registry sha256) converted with sharp to a lossless PNG at the registered size (58 re-exported by the release at another size are resized to the registered size so layouts do not move; the 48 `relic.<id>.iconN` sizes that only the old tree had are resized from the pack's `relic.<id>` image). Result on a clean tree: 631 + 893 images, 1530 of 1530 on disk, committed metas unchanged.
